Ground Truth: Let's calculate the clustering coefficient step by step.
For a directed graph, the clustering coefficient for a node u is T / (D * (D - 1)), where T is the number of edges between neighbors of u, and D is the out-degree of u.
Node 6's neighbors are [9, 10, 14, 5]. There are 5 edges between them: [(9, 10), (9, 5), (10, 9), (5, 9), (5, 14)].
Node 6's out-degree is 4.
So the the clustering coefficient of node 6 is 5 / (4 * (4 - 1)) =  <<<0.4167>>>

Sample 32 (ID: 9714, Task: clustering_coefficient)
Ground Truth: Let's calculate the clustering coefficient step by step.
For an undirected graph, the clustering coefficient for a node u is 2 * T / (D * (D - 1)), where T is the number of edges between neighbors of u, and D is the degree of u.
Node 5's neighbors are [2, 0, 8, 7]. There are 2 edges between them: [(2, 0), (2, 8)].
Node 5's degree is 4.
So the the clustering coefficient of node 5 is 2 * 2 / (4 * (4 - 1)) =  <<<0.3333>>>
